266: +package core
267: +
268: +import (
269: +	"fmt"
270: +	"strings"
271: +
272: +	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
273: +	"gopkg.in/yaml.v2"
274: +)
275: +
276: +// labRuntimeTopologyWithExpandedMagicVars renders the per-node values that local runtimes
277: +// normally resolve while initializing nodes. Inherited fields are materialized on each node so
278: +// that defaults, kinds, and groups can contain node-scoped variables too.
279: +func (c *CLab) labRuntimeTopologyWithExpandedMagicVars() ([]byte, error) {
280: +	if !strings.Contains(string(c.renderedTopology), "__clab") {
281: +		return c.renderedTopology, nil
282: +	}
283: +
284: +	source := &Config{}
285: +	if err := yaml.Unmarshal(c.renderedTopology, source); err != nil {
286: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
287: +			"failed to parse rendered topology for magic variable expansion: %w",
288: +			err,
289: +		)
290: +	}
291: +	if source.Topology == nil {
292: +		return c.renderedTopology, nil
293: +	}
294: +
295: +	expanded := &Config{}
296: +	if err := yaml.Unmarshal(c.renderedTopology, expanded); err != nil {
297: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
298: +			"failed to copy rendered topology for magic variable expansion: %w",
299: +			err,
300: +		)
301: +	}
302: +
303: +	for nodeName := range source.Topology.Nodes {
304: +		if err := c.expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars(
305: +			source.Topology,
306: +			expanded.Topology,
307: +			nodeName,
308: +		); err != nil {
309: +			return nil, err
310: +		}
311: +	}
312: +	clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields(expanded.Topology)
313: +
314: +	var document yaml.MapSlice
315: +	if err := yaml.Unmarshal(c.renderedTopology, &document); err != nil {
316: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse rendered topology document: %w", err)
317: +	}
318: +	for i := range document {
319: +		if document[i].Key == "topology" {
320: +			document[i].Value = expanded.Topology
321: +			break
322: +		}
323: +	}
324: +
325: +	result, err := yaml.Marshal(document)
326: +	if err != nil {
327: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to render topology with expanded magic variables: %w", err)
328: +	}
329: +
330: +	return result, nil
331: +}
332: +
333: +func (c *CLab) expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars(
334: +	source, expanded *clabtypes.Topology,
335: +	nodeName string,
336: +) error {
337: +	node := expanded.Nodes[nodeName]
338: +	if node == nil {
339: +		node = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{}
340: +		expanded.Nodes[nodeName] = node
341: +	}
342: +
343: +	r := c.magicVarReplacer(nodeName)
344: +	node.StartupConfig = r.Replace(source.GetNodeStartupConfig(nodeName))
345: +
346: +	var err error
347: +	node.Binds, err = source.GetNodeBinds(nodeName)
348: +	if err != nil {
349: +		return fmt.Errorf(
350: +			"failed to resolve binds for node %q during magic variable expansion: %w",
351: +			nodeName,
352: +			err,
353: +		)
354: +	}
355: +	for i := range node.Binds {
356: +		parts := strings.Split(node.Binds[i], ":")
357: +		parts[0] = r.Replace(parts[0])
358: +		node.Binds[i] = strings.Join(parts, ":")
359: +	}
360: +
361: +	node.Volumes, err = source.GetNodeVolumes(nodeName)
362: +	if err != nil {
363: +		return fmt.Errorf(
364: +			"failed to resolve volumes for node %q during magic variable expansion: %w",
365: +			nodeName,
366: +			err,
367: +		)
368: +	}
369: +	for i := range node.Volumes {
370: +		parts := strings.Split(node.Volumes[i], ":")
371: +		if len(parts) == 1 {
372: +			continue
373: +		}
374: +		parts[0] = r.Replace(parts[0])
375: +		node.Volumes[i] = strings.Join(parts, ":")
376: +	}
377: +
378: +	node.Exec = source.GetNodeExec(nodeName)
379: +	if labRuntimeNodeHasStages(source, nodeName) {
380: +		node.Stages, err = source.GetStages(nodeName)
381: +		if err != nil {
382: +			return fmt.Errorf(
383: +				"failed to resolve stages for node %q during magic variable expansion: %w",
384: +				nodeName,
385: +				err,
386: +			)
387: +		}
388: +	}
389: +	nodeConfig := &clabtypes.NodeConfig{
390: +		ShortName: nodeName,
391: +		Exec:      node.Exec,
392: +		Stages:    node.Stages,
393: +	}
394: +	c.processNodeExecs(nodeConfig)
395: +	node.Exec = nodeConfig.Exec
396: +	node.Stages = nodeConfig.Stages
397: +
398: +	entries := c.expandKindSpecificConfigMagicVars(
399: +		source.GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName), nodeName,
400: +	)
401: +	if len(entries) != 0 {
402: +		node.KindSpecificConfig = make(map[string]any, len(entries))
403: +		for _, entry := range entries {
404: +			node.KindSpecificConfig[entry.Key] = entry.Value
405: +		}
406: +	}
407: +
408: +	return nil
409: +}
410: +
411: +func labRuntimeNodeHasStages(topology *clabtypes.Topology, nodeName string) bool {
412: +	definitions := []*clabtypes.NodeDefinition{
413: +		topology.Defaults,
414: +		topology.GetKind(topology.GetNodeKind(nodeName)),
415: +		topology.GetGroup(topology.GetNodeGroup(nodeName)),
416: +		topology.Nodes[nodeName],
417: +	}
418: +	for _, definition := range definitions {
419: +		if definition != nil && definition.Stages != nil {
420: +			return true
421: +		}
422: +	}
423: +
424: +	return false
425: +}
426: +
427: +// clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields prevents the inherited values from being merged a second
428: +// time after their effective, expanded copies have been placed on every node.
429: +func clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields(topology *clabtypes.Topology) {
430: +	definitions := []*clabtypes.NodeDefinition{topology.Defaults}
431: +	for _, definition := range topology.Kinds {
432: +		definitions = append(definitions, definition)
433: +	}
434: +	for _, definition := range topology.Groups {
435: +		definitions = append(definitions, definition)
436: +	}
437: +
438: +	for _, definition := range definitions {
439: +		if definition == nil {
440: +			continue
441: +		}
442: +		definition.StartupConfig = ""
443: +		definition.Binds = nil
444: +		definition.Volumes = nil
445: +		definition.Exec = nil
446: +		definition.Stages = nil
447: +		definition.KindSpecificConfig = nil
448: +	}
449: +}

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

<path>/source/head/core/labruntime_topology.go</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"fmt"
5: 	"strings"
6: 
7: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
8: 	"gopkg.in/yaml.v2"
9: )
10: 
11: // labRuntimeTopologyWithExpandedMagicVars renders the per-node values that local runtimes
12: // normally resolve while initializing nodes. Inherited fields are materialized on each node so
13: // that defaults, kinds, and groups can contain node-scoped variables too.
14: func (c *CLab) labRuntimeTopologyWithExpandedMagicVars() ([]byte, error) {
15: 	if !strings.Contains(string(c.renderedTopology), "__clab") {
16: 		return c.renderedTopology, nil
17: 	}
18: 
19: 	source := &Config{}
20: 	if err := yaml.Unmarshal(c.renderedTopology, source); err != nil {
21: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
22: 			"failed to parse rendered topology for magic variable expansion: %w",
23: 			err,
24: 		)
25: 	}
26: 	if source.Topology == nil {
27: 		return c.renderedTopology, nil
28: 	}
29: 
30: 	expanded := &Config{}
31: 	if err := yaml.Unmarshal(c.renderedTopology, expanded); err != nil {
32: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
33: 			"failed to copy rendered topology for magic variable expansion: %w",
34: 			err,
35: 		)
36: 	}
37: 
38: 	for nodeName := range source.Topology.Nodes {
39: 		if err := c.expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars(
40: 			source.Topology,
41: 			expanded.Topology,
42: 			nodeName,
43: 		); err != nil {
44: 			return nil, err
45: 		}
46: 	}
47: 	clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields(expanded.Topology)
48: 
49: 	var document yaml.MapSlice
50: 	if err := yaml.Unmarshal(c.renderedTopology, &document); err != nil {
51: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse rendered topology document: %w", err)
52: 	}
53: 	for i := range document {
54: 		if document[i].Key == "topology" {
55: 			document[i].Value = expanded.Topology
56: 			break
57: 		}
58: 	}
59: 
60: 	result, err := yaml.Marshal(document)
61: 	if err != nil {
62: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to render topology with expanded magic variables: %w", err)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	return result, nil
66: }
67: 
68: func (c *CLab) expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars(
69: 	source, expanded *clabtypes.Topology,
70: 	nodeName string,
71: ) error {
72: 	node := expanded.Nodes[nodeName]
73: 	if node == nil {
74: 		node = &clabtypes.NodeDefinition{}
75: 		expanded.Nodes[nodeName] = node
76: 	}
77: 
78: 	r := c.magicVarReplacer(nodeName)
79: 	node.StartupConfig = r.Replace(source.GetNodeStartupConfig(nodeName))
80: 
81: 	var err error
82: 	node.Binds, err = source.GetNodeBinds(nodeName)
83: 	if err != nil {
84: 		return fmt.Errorf(
85: 			"failed to resolve binds for node %q during magic variable expansion: %w",
86: 			nodeName,
87: 			err,
88: 		)
89: 	}
90: 	for i := range node.Binds {
91: 		parts := strings.Split(node.Binds[i], ":")
92: 		parts[0] = r.Replace(parts[0])
93: 		node.Binds[i] = strings.Join(parts, ":")
94: 	}
95: 
96: 	node.Volumes, err = source.GetNodeVolumes(nodeName)
97: 	if err != nil {
98: 		return fmt.Errorf(
99: 			"failed to resolve volumes for node %q during magic variable expansion: %w",
100: 			nodeName,
101: 			err,
102: 		)
103: 	}

147: func resolveRuntimeName(name string) string {
148: 	envN := os.Getenv("CLAB_RUNTIME")
149: 	log.Debugf("env runtime var value is %v", envN)
150: 
151: 	switch {
152: 	case name != "":
153: 		return strings.ToLower(name)
154: 	case envN != "":
155: 		return strings.ToLower(envN)
156: 	default:

794: 	// checks are skipped when, for example, the destroy operation is run
795: 	if !c.checkBindsPaths {
796: 		return nil
797: 	}

Reasoning
Interesting — in `expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars`, `expandKindSpecificConfigMagicVars` is called; but this function requires `c.TopoPaths != nil && TopologyFileIsSet()`. In the test `TestLabRuntimeTopologyPreservesLinksWhenExpandingMagicVars`, TopoPaths is set but TopologyFileIsSet is false, so entries returned as parsed — meaning KindSpecificConfig entries aren't expanded in that test but that's fine.

But in real flow, `prepareLabRuntimeTopology` — is TopoPaths set? Yes (WithTopoPath). OK.

However, there's a subtlety: `GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName)` merges defaults + kind + group + node KindSpecificConfig entries. When node's KindSpecificConfig is set to the merged entries map... wait, entries is a slice of {Key, Value}; the code builds map[Key]Value. But if the same key appears in both group and node with different values, merge order matters — GetNodeKindSpecificConfig presumably handles precedence. Let me check types.

Now important: KindSpecificConfig on a NodeDefinition is `map[string]any` keyed by kind. When materializing onto the node, the merged entries (keyed by kind name?) — hmm. GetNodeKindSpecificConfig returns entries filtered for the node's kind presumably. Setting node.KindSpecificConfig[key]=value where key is the kind name — matches structure. OK.

Reasoning
Now important checks:

1. `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` — GetStages never returns nil (it always returns a non-nil Stages with InitDefaults). So the guard `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` is needed to avoid setting Stages on nodes that didn't have stages. But wait — the check checks defaults, kind, group, node for non-nil Stages. If none has stages, node.Stages stays nil. Good. But if GetStages were called directly it would always create defaults. So the guard makes sense. But is there an existing helper? Not exactly. The helper duplicates the level-iteration logic that GetStages does internally. Minor duplication; P4 at most.

2. Volumes skip len(parts)==1 — mirrors `resolveVolumePaths`. Binds: mirrors `resolveBindPaths` which replaces elems[0] always (even len 1). OK — actually the asymmetry matches existing behavior in resolveBindPaths/resolveVolumePaths. Good — consistent reuse.

Wait, but there's a subtle bug in the volumes loop: `if len(parts) == 1 { continue }` then replaces parts[0]. But `parts` could be len 1 for anonymous volume. OK matches existing.

But hmm, in binds loop there's no guard; fine.

3. What about the runtime merging behavior with cleared fields — the `labruntime` package's merge logic. Does the clabernetes runtime merge node definitions from defaults/kinds/groups (e.g., `mergeNodeDefinitions`)? If the runtime performs its own inheritance merge, then clearing those inherited fields means the runtime no longer merges other inherited fields? No — only the six fields are cleared; other fields remain and merge as before. But: by materializing e.g. Binds on every node, when the runtime merges, node-level binds now override... well they were already overridden identically. Actually one subtlety: bind merging keyed by destination — if defaults has bind to dst X and node has bind to dst Y, the materialized node has both X and Y; runtime merge would previously combine them too. Equivalent.

But another subtlety: `GetNodeBinds` resolves binds via `NewBindFromString` and re-`String()` — this may canonicalize binds, e.g., expanding relative paths? Let me check NewBindFromString — does it resolve `~` or relative paths? Probably not. The materialized binds in the new topology are canonicalized strings. Also possible change: binds list sorted. Equivalent set.

However — a real behavioral concern: previously (base), `renderedTopology` contained the raw rendered YAML, and the runtime did its own inheritance. Now for the magic-var path, we inject fully merged per-node fields. But only when `__clab` appears. If magic vars appear only in, say, `defaults.env` (like `MYVAR=__clabNodeName__`), the fast path triggers (contains "__clab"), expansion runs, but env is not among the six fields — env stays at defaults level with literal `__clabNodeName__`. The result is same as base (no expansion), so not a regression. But the six materialized fields are now also cleared from inherited levels even though their content may not have contained magic vars — consistent with merging semantics.

Hmm wait, actually there's a subtle issue with `clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields` clearing `KindSpecificConfig` on defaults: KindSpecificConfig on the defaults level is keyed by kind and can apply to nodes of that kind. The materialization via `GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName)` — does it merge the kind-specific config from defaults/kind/group for the node's kind? Let me check line 1195.

4. A potentially significant issue: `GetNodeBinds` uses `NewBindFromString` which may error on binds that don't parse (e.g., bind with no colon → error "invalid bind format"?). If a bind string previously passed through the runtime as raw... Actually the lab runtime presumably also parses binds the same way. Not new.

5. Another concern: `labRuntimeTopologyWithExpandedMagicVars` uses `strings.Contains("__clab")` — this triggers the expensive path and comment/format loss for any topology containing `__clab` even in comments. Minor (P4). But also: after expansion the output is re-marshaled — the links round trip requires correct marshaling of link types (hence link.go changes). The test `TestLabRuntimeTopologyPreservesLinksWhenExpandingMagicVars` covers brief, host, mgmt-net, vxlan-stitch. But what about other link types: `LinkVEthRaw` with `type: veth`, `LinkVxlanRaw` plain (type: vxlan), macvlan, dummy, mgmt-net... the test covers host, mgmt-net, vxlan-stitch, brief veth. Not plain vxlan, not macvlan, not dummy, not veth with explicit type. Potential marshaling bugs in those cases? Since marshal cases exist for each, and the fix touched host and vxlan only. Let me examine the full MarshalYAML and UnmarshalYAML to check whether other cases have wrong Type or fields lost (e.g., the `Type` injection for veth case with comment "Type field is injected artificially").

Key: LinkVxlanRaw now has `LinkType yaml:"-"`. When a `type: vxlan-stitch` link is unmarshaled, LinkType must be set from the `type` field by LinkDefinition's UnmarshalYAML. Let me read links/link.go and link_vxlan.go in head.

287: // MarshalYAML serializes LinkDefinition (e.g when used with generate command).
288: // As of now it falls back to converting the LinkConfig into a
289: // RawVEthLink, such that the generated LinkConfigs adhere to the new LinkDefinition
290: // format instead of the brief one.
291: func (r *LinkDefinition) MarshalYAML() (any, error) {
292: 	switch r.Link.GetType() {
293: 	case LinkTypeHost:
294: 		x := struct {
295: 			LinkHostRaw `yaml:",inline"`
296: 			Type        string `yaml:"type"`
297: 		}{
298: 			LinkHostRaw: *r.Link.(*LinkHostRaw),
299: 			Type:        string(LinkTypeHost),
300: 		}
301: 		return x, nil
302: 	case LinkTypeVEth:
303: 		x := struct {
304: 			// the Type field is injected artificially
305: 			// to allow strict yaml parsing to work.
306: 			Type        string `yaml:"type"`
307: 			LinkVEthRaw `yaml:",inline"`
308: 		}{
309: 			LinkVEthRaw: *r.Link.(*LinkVEthRaw),
310: 			Type:        string(LinkTypeVEth),
311: 		}
312: 		return x, nil
313: 	case LinkTypeVethStitch:
314: 		x := struct {
315: 			Type                string `yaml:"type"`
316: 			LinkVEthStitchedRaw `yaml:",inline"`
317: 		}{
318: 			LinkVEthStitchedRaw: *r.Link.(*LinkVEthStitchedRaw),
319: 			Type:                string(LinkTypeVethStitch),
320: 		}
321: 		return x, nil
322: 	case LinkTypeMgmtNet:
323: 		x := struct {
324: 			Type           string `yaml:"type"`
325: 			LinkMgmtNetRaw `yaml:",inline"`
326: 		}{
327: 			LinkMgmtNetRaw: *r.Link.(*LinkMgmtNetRaw),
328: 			Type:           string(LinkTypeMgmtNet),
329: 		}
330: 		return x, nil
331: 	case LinkTypeMacVLan:
332: 		x := struct {
333: 			Type           string `yaml:"type"`
334: 			LinkMacVlanRaw `yaml:",inline"`
335: 		}{
336: 			LinkMacVlanRaw: *r.Link.(*LinkMacVlanRaw),
337: 			Type:           string(LinkTypeMacVLan),
338: 		}
339: 		return x, nil
340: 	case LinkTypeVxlan:
341: 		raw := r.Link.(*LinkVxlanRaw)
342: 		linkType := raw.LinkType
343: 		if linkType == "" {
344: 			linkType = LinkTypeVxlan
345: 		}
346: 		x := struct {
347: 			Type         string `yaml:"type"`
348: 			LinkVxlanRaw `yaml:",inline"`
349: 		}{
350: 			LinkVxlanRaw: *raw,
351: 			Type:         string(linkType),
352: 		}
353: 		return x, nil
354: 	case LinkTypeDummy:
355: 		x := struct {
356: 			Type         string `yaml:"type"`
357: 			LinkDummyRaw `yaml:",inline"`
358: 		}{
359: 			LinkDummyRaw: *r.Link.(*LinkDummyRaw),
360: 			Type:         string(LinkTypeDummy),
361: 		}
362: 		return x, nil
363: 	case LinkTypeBrief:
364: 		return r.Link, nil
365: 	}
366: 
367: 	return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to marshall")
368: }
369: 
370: // RawLink is an interface that all raw link types must implement.
371: // Raw link types define the links as they are defined in the topology file
372: // and solely a product of unmarshaling.
373: // Raw links are later "resolved" to concrete link types (e.g LinkVeth).
374: type RawLink interface {
375: 	Resolve(params *ResolveParams) (Link, error)
376: 	GetType() LinkType
377: }
378: 
379: type filteredLinkCleaner interface {
380: 	cleanupFilteredLink(context.Context, string, []string) error
381: }
382: 
383: // CleanupFilteredLinks lets raw link types remove resources that cannot be
384: // reached through resolved links after a node filter excludes one endpoint.
385: func CleanupFilteredLinks(
386: 	ctx context.Context,
387: 	linkDefs []*LinkDefinition,
388: 	labName string,
389: 	nodesFilter []string,
390: ) error {
391: 	if len(nodesFilter) == 0 {
392: 		return nil
393: 	}
394: 
395: 	for i, linkDef := range linkDefs {
396: 		if linkDef == nil {
397: 			continue
398: 		}
399: 		cleaner, ok := linkDef.Link.(filteredLinkCleaner)
400: 		if !ok {
401: 			continue
402: 		}
403: 		if err := cleaner.cleanupFilteredLink(ctx, labName, nodesFilter); err != nil {
404: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed cleaning up filtered link %d: %w", i, err)
405: 		}
406: 	}
407: 
408: 	return nil
409: }
410: 
411: // Link is an interface that all concrete link types must implement.
412: // Concrete link types are resolved from raw links and become part of CLab.Links.
413: type Link interface {
414: 	// Deploy deploys the link. Endpoint is the endpoint that triggers the creation of the link.
415: 	Deploy(context.Context, Endpoint) error
416: 	// PostDeploy runs link work that must happen after endpoint deployment.
417: 	PostDeploy(context.Context) error
418: 	// Remove removes the link.
419: 	Remove(context.Context) error
420: 	// GetType returns the type of the link.
421: 	GetType() LinkType
422: 	// GetEndpoints returns the endpoints of the link.
423: 	GetEndpoints() []Endpoint
424: 	// GetRuntimeEndpoints returns endpoints apply/deploy may create, discover, or remove.
425: 	// Parent-only and remote metadata endpoints are excluded where applicable.
426: 	GetRuntimeEndpoints() []Endpoint
427: 	// GetMTU returns the Link MTU.
428: 	GetMTU() int
429: 	// GetVars returns the link-level vars.
430: 	GetVars() map[string]any
431: }
432: 
433: func extractHostNodeInterfaceData(
434: 	lb *LinkBriefRaw,
435: 	specialEPIndex int,
436: ) (host, hostIf, node, nodeIf string, err error) {
437: 	// the index of the node is the specialEndpointIndex +1  modulo 2
438: 	nodeindex := (specialEPIndex + 1) % 2
439: 
440: 	hostData := strings.SplitN(lb.Endpoints[specialEPIndex], ":", 2)
441: 	nodeData := strings.SplitN(lb.Endpoints[nodeindex], ":", 2)
442: 
443: 	if len(hostData) != 2 {
444: 		return "", "", "", "",
445: 			fmt.Errorf(
446: 				"invalid link endpoint format. expected <node>:<port>, got %s",
447: 				lb.Endpoints[specialEPIndex],
448: 			)
449: 	}
450: 
451: 	if len(nodeData) != 2 {
452: 		return "", "", "", "",
453: 			fmt.Errorf(
454: 				"invalid link endpoint format. expected <node>:<port>, got %s",
455: 				lb.Endpoints[nodeindex],
456: 			)
457: 	}
458: 
459: 	host = hostData[0]
460: 	hostIf = hostData[1]
461: 	node = nodeData[0]
462: 	nodeIf = nodeData[1]
463: 
464: 	log.Debugf(
465: 		"extractHostNodeInterfaceData: eps=%v specialIndex=%d -> host=%s hostIf=%s node=%s nodeIf=%s",
466: 		lb.Endpoints,
467: 		specialEPIndex,
468: 		host,
469: 		hostIf,
470: 		node,
471: 		nodeIf,
472: 	)
473: 	return host, hostIf, node, nodeIf, nil
474: }
475: 
476: func mapBriefVarsToEndpoints(lb *LinkBriefRaw, endpoints []*EndpointRaw) error {
477: 	if len(lb.LinkCommonParams.IPv4) > 0 {
478: 		if err := parseVarIPBrief("ipv4", lb.LinkCommonParams.IPv4, endpoints); err != nil {
479: 			return err
480: 		}
481: 	}
482: 
483: 	// Map IPv6 addresses from link-level to endpoints
484: 	if len(lb.LinkCommonParams.IPv6) > 0 {
485: 		if err := parseVarIPBrief("ipv6", lb.LinkCommonParams.IPv6, endpoints); err != nil {
486: 			return err
487: 		}
488: 	}
489: 	return nil
490: }
491: 
492: func parseVarIPBrief(af string, vals []string, endpoints []*EndpointRaw) error {
493: 	nVals := len(vals)
494: 
495: 	if nVals == 0 {
496: 		return nil
497: 	} else if nVals > len(endpoints) {
498: 		return fmt.Errorf("number of defined %s vars exceed number of endpoints", af)
499: 	}
500: 
501: 	for i, cidr := range vals {
502: 		cidr = strings.TrimSpace(cidr)
503: 		if cidr == "" {
504: 			// empty entry = no addr on this interface
505: 			continue
506: 		}
507: 		prefix, err := netip.ParsePrefix(cidr)
508: 		if err != nil {
509: 			return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %s var has invalid prefix %q (%v)", af, cidr, err)
510: 		}
511: 		switch af {
512: 		case "ipv4":
513: 			if !prefix.Addr().Is4() {
514: 				return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %s var has non-IPv4 prefix %q", af, prefix)
515: 			}
516: 			endpoints[i].IPv4 = cidr
517: 		case "ipv6":
518: 			if !prefix.Addr().Is6() {
519: 				return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %s var has non-IPv6 prefix %q", af, prefix)
520: 			}
521: 			endpoints[i].IPv6 = cidr
522: 		}
523: 	}
524: 	return nil
525: }
526: 
527: func genRandomIfName() string {
528: 	return "clab-" + genRandomString(8)
529: }
530: 
531: func genRandomString(length int) string {
532: 	s, _ := uuid.New().MarshalText() // .MarshalText() always return a nil error
533: 	return string(s[:length])
534: }

536: // Node interface is an interface that is satisfied by all nodes.
537: // It is used a subset of the nodes.Node interface and is used to pass nodes.Nodes
538: // to the link resolver without causing a circular dependency.
539: type Node interface {
540: 	// AddLinkToContainer adds a link to the node (container).
541: 	// In case of a regular container, it will push the link into the
542: 	// network namespace and then run the function f within the namespace
543: 	// this is to rename the link, set mtu, set the interface up, e.g. see
544: 	// link.SetNameMACAndUpInterface()
545: 	//
546: 	// In case of a bridge node (ovs or regular linux bridge) it will take the interface and make
547: 	// the bridge
548: 	// the master of the interface and bring the interface up.
549: 	AddLinkToContainer(ctx context.Context, link netlink.Link, f func(ns.NetNS) error) error
550: 	// AddEndpoint attaches an endpoint discovered from topology resolution and may normalize
551: 	// endpoint identity first, such as interface-name remapping.
552: 	AddEndpoint(e Endpoint) error
553: 	AdoptEndpoint(e Endpoint) error
554: 	ReleaseEndpoint(e Endpoint) error
555: 	GetLinkEndpointType() LinkEndpointType
556: 	GetShortName() string
557: 	GetEndpoints() []Endpoint
558: 	ExecFunction(context.Context, func(ns.NetNS) error) error
559: 	GetState() clabnodesstate.NodeState
560: 	Delete(ctx context.Context) error
561: }

15: const (
16: 	// vxlan port is different from the default port number 4789
17: 	// since 4789 may be filtered by the firewalls or clash with other overlay services.
18: 	VxLANDefaultPort = 14789
19: )
20: 
21: // LinkVxlanRaw is the raw (string) representation of a vxlan link as defined in the topology file.
22: type LinkVxlanRaw struct {
23: 	LinkCommonParams `            yaml:",inline"`
24: 	Remote           string      `yaml:"remote"`
25: 	VNI              int         `yaml:"vni"`
26: 	Endpoint         EndpointRaw `yaml:"endpoint"`
27: 	UdpPort          int         `yaml:"udp-port,omitempty"` // deprecated
28: 	DstPort          int         `yaml:"dst-port,omitempty"`
29: 	ParentInterface  string      `yaml:"parent-interface,omitempty"`
30: 	SrcPort          int         `yaml:"src-port,omitempty"`
31: 
32: 	// we use the same struct for vxlan and vxlan stitch, so we need to differentiate them in the
33: 	// raw format
34: 	LinkType LinkType `yaml:"-"`
35: }
36: 
37: func (lr *LinkVxlanRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams) (Link, error) {
38: 	switch lr.LinkType {
39: 	case LinkTypeVxlan:
40: 		return lr.resolveVxlan(params, false)
41: 
42: 	case LinkTypeVxlanStitch:
43: 		return lr.resolveStitchedVxlan(params)
44: 
45: 	default:
46: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected LinkType %s for Vxlan based link", lr.LinkType)
47: 	}
48: }
49: 
50: func (lr *LinkVxlanRaw) ResolveStitched(params *ResolveParams) (*VxlanStitched, error) {
51: 	return lr.resolveStitchedVxlan(params)
52: }
53: 
54: // resolveStitchedVEthComponent creates the veth link and returns it, the endpoint that is
55: // supposed to be stitched is returned separately for further processing.
56: func (lr *LinkVxlanRaw) resolveStitchedVEthComponent(
57: 	params *ResolveParams,
58: ) (Link, Endpoint, error) {
59: 	var err error
60: 
61: 	// hostIface is the name of the host interface that will be created
62: 	hostIface := clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(
63: 		fmt.Sprintf("ve-%s_%s", lr.Endpoint.Node, lr.Endpoint.Iface),
64: 	)
65: 
66: 	// when tools vxlan create command is used, the hostIface is provided
67: 	// by the user, otherwise it is generated
68: 	if params.VxlanIfaceNameOverwrite != "" {
69: 		hostIface = clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(params.VxlanIfaceNameOverwrite)
70: 	}
71: 
72: 	lhr := &LinkHostRaw{
73: 		LinkCommonParams: lr.LinkCommonParams,
74: 		HostInterface:    hostIface,
75: 		Endpoint: &EndpointRaw{
76: 			Node:  lr.Endpoint.Node,
77: 			Iface: lr.Endpoint.Iface,
78: 		},
79: 	}
80: 
81: 	hl, err := lhr.Resolve(params)
82: 	if err != nil {
83: 		return nil, nil, err
84: 	}
85: 
86: 	if hl == nil {
87: 		return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve host veth component for vxlan-stitch")
88: 	}
89: 
90: 	eps := hl.GetEndpoints()
91: 	if len(eps) != 2 {
92: 		return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(
93: 			"expected host veth component to resolve to 2 endpoints, got %d",
94: 			len(eps),
95: 		)
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	stitchEp, ok := endpointByInterfaceName(eps, hostIface)
99: 	if !ok {
100: 		return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf(
101: 			"expected host veth component to include endpoint %q",
102: 			hostIface,
103: 		)
104: 	}
105: 
106: 	return hl, stitchEp, nil
107: }
108: 
109: func endpointByInterfaceName(endpoints []Endpoint, ifaceName string) (Endpoint, bool) {
110: 	for _, ep := range endpoints {
111: 		if ep != nil && ep.GetIfaceName() == ifaceName {
112: 			return ep, true
113: 		}
114: 	}
115: 	return nil, false
116: }
117: 
118: // resolveStitchedVxlan resolves the stitched raw vxlan link.
119: func (lr *LinkVxlanRaw) resolveStitchedVxlan(params *ResolveParams) (*VxlanStitched, error) {
120: 	// prepare the vxlan struct
121: 	vxlanLink, err := lr.resolveVxlan(params, true)
122: 	if err != nil {
123: 		return nil, err
124: 	}
125: 
126: 	// prepare the veth struct
127: 	vethLink, stitchEp, err := lr.resolveStitchedVEthComponent(params)
128: 	if err != nil {
129: 		return nil, err
130: 	}
131: 
132: 	// return the stitched vxlan link
133: 	vxlanStitchedLink := NewVxlanStitched(vxlanLink, vethLink, stitchEp)
134: 
135: 	return vxlanStitchedLink, nil
136: }
137: 
138: func (lr *LinkVxlanRaw) resolveVxlan(params *ResolveParams, stitched bool) (*LinkVxlan, error) {
139: 	var err error
140: 	link := &LinkVxlan{
141: 		LinkCommonParams: lr.LinkCommonParams,
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	// Normalize link vars to ensure JSON serialization compatibility
145: 	link.Vars = normalizeVars(link.Vars)
146: 
147: 	link.localEndpoint, err = lr.resolveLocalEndpoint(stitched, params, link)
148: 	if err != nil {
149: 		return nil, err
150: 	}
151: 
152: 	ip := net.ParseIP(lr.Remote)
153: 	// if the returned ip is nil, an error occurred.
154: 	// we consider, that we maybe have a textual hostname
155: 	// e.g. dns name so we try to resolve the string next
156: 	if ip == nil {
157: 		ips, err := net.LookupIP(lr.Remote)
158: 		if err != nil {
159: 			return nil, err
160: 		}
161: 
162: 		// always use the first address
163: 		if len(ips) == 0 {
164: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to resolve %s", lr.Remote)
165: 		}
166: 
167: 		// prepare log message
168: 		sb := strings.Builder{}
169: 		for _, ip := range ips {
170: 			sb.WriteString(", ")
171: 			sb.WriteString(ip.String())
172: 		}
173: 		log.Debugf("looked up hostname %s, received IP addresses [%s]", lr.Remote, sb.String()[2:])
174: 		ip = ips[0]
175: 	}
176: 
177: 	parentIf := lr.ParentInterface
178: 
179: 	if parentIf == "" {
180: 		r, err := clabutils.GetRouteForIP(ip)
181: 		if err != nil {
182: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to find a route to VxLAN remote address %s", ip.String())
183: 		}
184: 
185: 		parentIf = r.Interface.Name
186: 	}
187: 
188: 	// resolve remote endpoint
189: 	link.remoteEndpoint = NewEndpointVxlan(params.Nodes["host"], link)
190: 	link.remoteEndpoint.parentIface = parentIf
191: 	// If the deprecated udp-port is used in the topology, copy it over to the dst-port
192: 	if lr.UdpPort != 0 && lr.DstPort == 0 {
193: 		lr.DstPort = lr.UdpPort
194: 	}
195: 	link.remoteEndpoint.dstPort = lr.DstPort
196: 	link.remoteEndpoint.srcPort = lr.SrcPort
197: 	if lr.DstPort == 0 {
198: 		link.remoteEndpoint.dstPort = VxLANDefaultPort
199: 	}
200: 	link.remoteEndpoint.remote = ip
201: 	link.remoteEndpoint.vni = lr.VNI
202: 	// check if MAC-Addr is set in the raw vxlan link
203: 	if lr.Endpoint.MAC == "" {
204: 		// if it is not set generate a MAC
205: 		link.remoteEndpoint.MAC, err = clabutils.GenMac(clabconstants.ClabOUI)
206: 		if err != nil {
207: 			return nil, err
208: 		}
209: 	} else {
210: 		// if a MAC is set, parse and use it
211: 		hwaddr, err := net.ParseMAC(lr.Endpoint.MAC)
212: 		if err != nil {
213: 			return nil, err
214: 		}
215: 		link.remoteEndpoint.MAC = hwaddr
216: 	}
217: 
218: 	return link, nil
219: }
220: 
221: func (lr *LinkVxlanRaw) resolveLocalEndpoint(
222: 	stitched bool,
223: 	params *ResolveParams,
224: 	link *LinkVxlan,
225: ) (Endpoint, error) {
226: 	if stitched {
227: 		// point the vxlan endpoint to the host system
228: 		vxlanRawEp := lr.Endpoint
229: 		vxlanRawEp.Iface = clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(
230: 			fmt.Sprintf("vx-%s_%s", lr.Endpoint.Node, lr.Endpoint.Iface),
231: 		)
232: 
233: 		if params.VxlanIfaceNameOverwrite != "" {
234: 			vxlanRawEp.Iface = clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(
235: 				fmt.Sprintf("vx-%s", params.VxlanIfaceNameOverwrite),
236: 			)
237: 		}
238: 
239: 		// in the stitched vxlan mode we create vxlan interface in the host node namespace
240: 		vxlanRawEp.Node = "host"
241: 		vxlanRawEp.MAC = ""
242: 
243: 		// resolve local Endpoint
244: 		return vxlanRawEp.Resolve(params, link)
245: 	} else {
246: 		// resolve local Endpoint
247: 		return lr.Endpoint.Resolve(params, link)
248: 	}
249: }
250: 
251: func (*LinkVxlanRaw) GetType() LinkType {
252: 	return LinkTypeVxlan
253: }
254: 
255: type LinkVxlan struct {
256: 	LinkCommonParams
257: 	localEndpoint  Endpoint
258: 	remoteEndpoint *EndpointVxlan
259: }
260: 
261: func (l *LinkVxlan) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ Endpoint) error {
262: 	err := l.deployVxlanInterface()
263: 	if err != nil {
264: 		return err
265: 	}
266: 
267: 	// retrieve the Link by name
268: 	mvInterface, err := netlink.LinkByName(l.localEndpoint.GetRandIfaceName())
269: 	if err != nil {
270: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to lookup %q: %v", l.localEndpoint.GetRandIfaceName(), err)
271: 	}
272: 
273: 	// add the link to the Node Namespace
274: 	err = l.localEndpoint.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, mvInterface,
275: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(mvInterface, l.localEndpoint))
276: 	return err
277: }
278: 
279: func (*LinkVxlan) PostDeploy(context.Context) error {
280: 	return nil
281: }
282: 
283: // deployVxlanInterface internal function to create the vxlan interface in the host namespace.
284: func (l *LinkVxlan) deployVxlanInterface() error {
285: 	// retrieve the parent interface netlink handle
286: 	parentIface, err := netlink.LinkByName(l.remoteEndpoint.parentIface)
287: 	if err != nil {
288: 		return fmt.Errorf(
289: 			"error looking up vxlan parent interface %s: %w",
290: 			l.remoteEndpoint.parentIface,
291: 			err,
292: 		)
293: 	}
294: 
295: 	// create the Vxlan struct
296: 	vxlanconf := netlink.Vxlan{
297: 		LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{
298: 			Name:         l.localEndpoint.GetRandIfaceName(),
299: 			TxQLen:       1000,
300: 			HardwareAddr: l.remoteEndpoint.MAC,
301: 		},
302: 		VxlanId:      l.remoteEndpoint.vni,
303: 		VtepDevIndex: parentIface.Attrs().Index,
304: 		Group:        l.remoteEndpoint.remote,
305: 		Learning:     true,
306: 	}
307: 	// set the destination UDP port
308: 	if l.remoteEndpoint.dstPort != 0 {
309: 		vxlanconf.Port = l.remoteEndpoint.dstPort
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	// set the source UDP port
313: 	if l.remoteEndpoint.srcPort != 0 {
314: 		vxlanconf.PortLow = l.remoteEndpoint.srcPort
315: 		vxlanconf.PortHigh = l.remoteEndpoint.srcPort + 1
316: 	}
317: 
318: 	// define the MTU if defined in the input
319: 	if l.MTU != 0 {
320: 		vxlanconf.LinkAttrs.MTU = l.MTU
321: 	}
322: 
323: 	// add the link
324: 	err = netlink.LinkAdd(&vxlanconf)
325: 	if err != nil {
326: 		return fmt.Errorf("error adding vxlan link %s: %w", l.localEndpoint.String(), err)
327: 	}
328: 
329: 	// fetch the mtu from the actual state for templated config generation
330: 	if l.MTU == 0 {
331: 		interf, err := netlink.LinkByName(l.localEndpoint.GetRandIfaceName())
332: 		if err != nil {
333: 			return fmt.Errorf(
334: 				"error looking up local vxlan endpoint of %s : %w",
335: 				l.localEndpoint.String(),
336: 				err,
337: 			)
338: 		}
339: 		l.MTU = interf.Attrs().MTU
340: 	}
341: 
342: 	return nil
343: }
344: 
345: func (l *LinkVxlan) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
346: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
347: 		return nil
348: 	}
349: 	err := l.localEndpoint.Remove(ctx)
350: 	if err != nil {
351: 		log.Debug(err)
352: 	}
353: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateRemoved
354: 	return nil
355: }
356: 
357: func (l *LinkVxlan) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
358: 	return []Endpoint{l.localEndpoint, l.remoteEndpoint}
359: }
360: 
361: func (l *LinkVxlan) GetRuntimeEndpoints() []Endpoint {
362: 	return []Endpoint{l.localEndpoint}
363: }
364: 
365: func (*LinkVxlan) GetType() LinkType {
366: 	return LinkTypeVxlan
367: }

Check docs mentioning magic vars limitations — docs/manual/topo-def-file.md has a magic variables section. Does it mention lab runtimes? Possibly there's a statement like "magic variables are not supported with lab runtimes" that's now stale. Let me grep docs for `__clabNodeName__` and lab runtime mentions.

q) Tests: labruntime_test.go adds good tests. But `TestLabRuntimeDeployRequestExpandsMagicVars` uses `WithRuntime(ClabernetesRuntimeName, nil)` — meaning c.LabRuntime set. OK.

r) Now check `Config` type in core — `runtimeConfig := &Config{}` in tests — that's core.Config? In core package, `Config` — there's `types.Config`? The test uses `runtimeConfig := &Config{}` with `runtimeConfig.Topology.Nodes[...].StartupConfig` — NodeDefinition. In core package, `Config` refers to... let me check: core has `type Config` in clab.go? There's `c.Config *types.Config`. In core package tests, `Config` resolves to types.Config? No — package core; if types.Config imported, it's `clabtypes.Config`. Is there a local `Config` alias in core? labruntime.go references `clablabruntime.DeployRequest`. Hmm the test file uses `&Config{}` — must be an alias defined somewhere in core, e.g. `type Config = clabtypes.Config`. Let me grep. Not critical.

Wait — actually NodeDefinition has `StartupConfig`, `Exec`, `Binds`, `Volumes`, `Stages`, `KindSpecificConfig` fields. Good.

s) One more potential correctness issue in `labRuntimeTopologyWithExpandedMagicVars`: the nodes iteration uses `source.Topology.Nodes` — if `expanded.Topology.Nodes` lacks a node present in source? Same doc, same. OK.

t) Now the concern about `expanded` vs `source` mutation: `expanded.Nodes[nodeName]` may be nil if node key exists with null value (`r2: {}` gives empty NodeDefinition; `r2:` null gives nil). Handled by creating empty NodeDefinition. Good.

u) `clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields` — clears on defaults even if defaults had exec etc. But WAIT: what about the `stages` merging behavior of the runtime: if the runtime merges stages by merging per-stage fields (merge semantics), pre-materialized stages on the node replace defaults' stages. GetStages merge: node stages take precedence; Merge merges per-stage. Since GetStages produces the merged result, equivalent.

v) Now — a potentially REAL issue: `GetStages` calls `InitDefaults()` which fills unset fields with defaults (e.g., `Create` stage with default behavior?). Does the runtime merge do the same? If the local runtime merge sets stage defaults too, equivalent. If the runtime relies on unset stage fields meaning something, materialized defaults could change behavior. Hmm. Let me check what InitDefaults does and whether the labruntime package merges stages similarly. Let's look at labruntime package for merging of node definitions.

Actually — how does the lab runtime (clabernetes) handle topology inheritance? Let me grep in labruntime package.

w) Also check: does the `expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars` handle the case where magic vars exist in fields NOT materialized, at the NODE level? E.g., node-level `env: [FOO=__clabNodeName__]` — stays unexpanded (as base). Pre-existing limitation, not a regression. But the PR's expansion of exec could be seen as inconsistent with env. Coverage note.

x) Potential duplicate: `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` checks `topology.GetKind(topology.GetNodeKind(nodeName))` etc. — duplicates GetStages internals. P4 optional simplification: could try GetStages and check if anything set... but InitDefaults makes GetStages always non-nil, so the guard is needed. Reasoning
So the clabernetes runtime uses `config.Topology.GetNodeStartupConfig(nodeName)`, `GetNodeBinds`, `GetNodeKindSpecificConfig`, and presumably `GetStages`/`GetNodeExec` for staging config maps and execs. It receives `TopologyDefinition` and parses it into `clabRuntimeConfig` with `Topology`. If the expanded topology now has per-node materialized fields with inherited fields cleared, then:
- `GetNodeStartupConfig` returns the expanded per-node value (correct).
- Before this PR, GetNodeStartupConfig at the runtime would merge defaults + node — but the defaults value would still contain `__clabNodeName__` unexpanded (as the runtime doesn't expand magic vars; it stages files). Did the runtime expand magic vars itself? Search clabernetes for magicVar or __clab.

Reasoning
So the clabernetes runtime already expands magic variables in startup-config paths via `replaceClabPathVariables` (called from resolveNodeFileReference presumably). The PR now expands them locally before sending. Double expansion is idempotent — fine.

But wait — a possible semantic conflict: `__clabDir__` and `__clabNodeDir__`. Locally, `c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()` and `c.TopoPaths.NodeDir(nodeName)`. In clabernetes, `topologyLabDir` is `req.TopologyLabDir` (same value passed by `labRuntimeDeployRequest` — `c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()`), so same. `__clabLabName__` → `filepath.Base(topologyLabDir)` both = "clab-lab1". Consistent.

Now, what about `binds`? Does clabernetes expand magic vars in binds? Grep for replaceClabPathVariables usage.

step-start
{
  "type": "step-start",
  "id": "prt_1e042c14f001426Ve8R8rEWIEY",
  "sessionID": "ses_e1fbf2cbdffemLun4D6iuiPxnZ",
  "messageID": "msg_1e042b8220017h1aqpUwhzzbGe"
}
Reasoning
The clabernetes compiler is an external dependency (github.com/clabernetes/clabernetes/compiler) — that's the remote compiler that renders node specs, and it handles execs/stages remotely (we can't inspect it here, external module). So the PR's local expansion of execs + stages changes what the remote compiler receives: exec commands now have magic vars pre-expanded. If the remote compiler also expands them (using its own paths, which may differ from local TopoPaths... e.g., `__clabDir__` on the c9s side may resolve differently — actually the c9s side may not have the local filesystem context at all; the DeployRequest carries TopologyLabDir so remote could compute same values). Double expansion is harmless. But there's a subtlety: `processNodeExecs` — does the compiler expect raw execs? Unknown; external. Coverage note.

Now, the `clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields` clearing on `expanded` — is this compatible with the remote compiler's inheritance merge? The remote compiler presumably does its own inheritance merge (defaults→kinds→groups→nodes). If it merges fields like `Exec` and `Binds` from defaults, clearing them locally is equivalent to materialization. But: does the c9s compiler support the magic variables itself for these fields? If yes, pre-expansion is harmless. If no (previously), inherited binds with `__clabNodeName__` would be passed through with the literal `__clabNodeName__` in all nodes' binds (same value for every node — broken). The PR fixes that by materializing per-node.

But here's a potential regression: `clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields` clears `KindSpecificConfig` from defaults/kinds/groups. The c9s compiler "treats kinds as opaque" (plan.go line 80 comment) — kind-specific config may be used by the compiler to configure node pods (e.g., `srl` kind config with `license`?). If the compiler merges KindSpecificConfig from kinds/groups itself, materialization per-node is equivalent as long as the merge keys match. GetNodeKindSpecificConfig merges node → group → kind → defaults with "first seen wins" (node precedence). Then the entries are re-keyed by kind name: `node.KindSpecificConfig[entry.Key] = entry.Value` — WAIT. This is a bug risk!

`GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName)` returns entries whose Key is the kind-specific config key — e.g., for an srl node, keys like `license`? Actually KindSpecificConfig on NodeDefinition is `map[string]any` where keys are kind names?? Let me check NodeDefinition and how KindSpecificConfig is parsed. In clab, topology yaml:

```yaml
topology:
  nodes:
    srl1:
      kind: srl
      srl: ...   # kind-specific key
```

Hmm, actually in the new clab, kind-specific config is defined under node definitions with kind name as key? Let me look at NodeDefinition and how KindSpecificConfig is populated during unmarshal. In types/node_definition.go there's probably custom UnmarshalYAML that moves unknown keys into KindSpecificConfig. Let me check.
